 succeeded in 0ms:
---
name: code-review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

On top of whatever the repo documents, the Standards axis always carries the **smell baseline** below — a fixed set of Fowler code smells (_Refactoring_, ch.3) that applies even when a repo documents nothing. Two rules bind it:

- **The repo overrides.** A documented repo standard always wins; where it endorses something the baseline would flag, suppress the smell.
- **Always a judgement call.** Each smell is a labelled heuristic ("possible Feature Envy"), never a hard violation — and, like any standard here, skip anything tooling already enforces.

Each smell reads *what it is* → *how to fix*; match it against the diff:

- **Mysterious Name** — a function, variable, or type whose name doesn't reveal what it does or holds. → rename it; if no honest name comes, the design's murky.
- **Duplicated Code** — the same logic shape appears in more than one hunk or file in the change. → extract the shared shape, call it from both.
- **Feature Envy** — a method that reaches into another object's data more than its own. → move the method onto the data it envies.
- **Data Clumps** — the same few fields or params keep travelling together (a type wanting to be born). → bundle them into one type, pass that.
- **Primitive Obsession** — a primitive or string standing in for a domain concept that deserves its own type. → give the concept its own small type.
- **Repeated Switches** — the same `switch`/`if`-cascade on the same type recurs across the change. → replace with polymorphism, or one map both sites share.
- **Shotgun Surgery** — one logical change forces scattered edits across many files in the diff. → gather what changes together into one module.
- **Divergent Change** — one file or module is edited for several unrelated reasons. → split so each module changes for one reason.
- **Speculative Generality** — abstraction, parameters, or hooks added for needs the spec doesn't have. → delete it; inline back until a real need shows.
- **Message Chains** — long `a.b().c().d()` navigation the caller shouldn't depend on. → hide the walk behind one method on the first object.
- **Middle Man** — a class or function that mostly just delegates onward. → cut it, call the real target direct.
- **Refused Bequest** — a subclass or implementer that ignores or overrides most of what it inherits. → drop the inheritance, use composition.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3, **plus the smell baseline from step 3** pasted in full — the sub-agent has no other access to it.
- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls — documented-standard breaches can be hard, but baseline smells are always judgement calls, and a documented repo standard overrides the baseline. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

# I05b Controller locality power-up: plan

Status: PLAN stage, 2026-11-02. Branch `claude/i05-power` from `origin/main` e56077bd. Source: "I05" and "Agent execution contract" in `docs/simulation-research-2026-11-01/report.md`; coordinator decision D12 in `docs/backlog-run-2026-11-01.html` (that file ends at D13 in this checkout; D14 is cited from the coordinator's task text, not found in the file, and the AMENDMENT quotes the scope as given rather than claiming a D14 clause). Builds on PR136 (`docs/controller-locality-2026-11-01/`, code in `packages/lineage/src/locality/`, `tools/locality/matrix.ts`, page `apps/lab/controller-locality/`). Exploratory, CPU only, no GPU, no physics/schema/golden change.

## 1. Bounded question

PR136 ended inconclusive (underpowered): M3 clusters 4 and 9 each had A = 1 improving edit of 24 assayed, against the pre-stated minimum of 5 (`RULE.minImproving`). Question: with enough assayed edits for the unchanged rule to be able to decide, what does it decide for the same two genomes, same operator, same neighbourhood families, same scenarios and thresholds? The slice does not ask anything new about controllers and does not change the rule. Output: a verdict from `decide()` plus a go/no-go for a separately versioned controller architecture, exploratory and bounded to these two genomes, the one-step operator, the 26,244-point probe grid and the three 16x16 tiny-world biomass assays.

## 2. Design decisions (to be fixed in AMENDMENT.md before any new assay)

1. **Unchanged:** genomes (M3 c4 sparse, M3 c9 dense; generalist is control only, does not vote), operator (`mutateInPlace` through `editFromSlots`), families (single6, single24, multi2, multi4), scenarios and `ASSAY` values except `seeds`, `tau` formula, gate (1000 / 250), `viableFloor`, `RULE` (deep-equality test against PR136's `protocolRecord().rule` and a pinned snapshot). No substitution of genomes: the panel carries singleton candidate lists (`sparse [4]`, `dense [9]`), so `runStudy` cannot fall through to c10 or try c16; if a genome fails the gate under the new seeds the verdict follows the existing rule (`measurement-limited` or single-genome `inconclusive`), pre-stated.
2. **Fixed N = 192 assayed genuine non-duplicate edits per M3 genome** (8 for the generalist, as before, only for the sensitivity and neutral controls and for tau). Power argument: PR136 observed A/N = 1/24 = 4.2% per genome. With A ~ Binomial(192, p): P(A >= 5) = 0.905 at p = 1/24; 0.805 at 3.5%; 0.685 at 3.0%; 0.340 at 2.0%; 0.991 at 6.0%. Both genomes need A >= 5: 0.82 at p = 1/24 for both (independent). N = 144 gives 0.72 per genome (0.52 both); N = 240 gives 0.97 (0.95) but is not affordable at the CPU budget. The AMENDMENT states these numbers and that power below p ~ 3% is low: a second inconclusive is a legitimate outcome and still reported. **What A >= 5 does and does not buy (review P1).** `decide()` is unchanged, so the amendment states which verdicts are reachable, with numbers: (a) A >= 5 on both genomes only moves the rule out of the `underpowered` guard, into `broadly-useful`, `entanglement-signal`, or the residual `inconclusive` (disagreement or neither rule met). That residual `inconclusive` is a decided outcome of the rule, distinct from the underpowered one, and is reported as such. (b) `broadly-useful` additionally needs K >= 10% of assayed genuine edits, i.e. K >= 20 at N = 192 (K >= 15 at 144); under the binomial model at p = 1/24 P(A >= 20) is about 0.0002 (0.0011 at N = 144), so a `broadly-useful` call is very unlikely unless the true improving rate is several times PR136's. (c) `entanglement-signal` needs median breadth (over all generated genuine edits, doubled units) >= 6 doubled (3 actuators) and >= null + 2 on both genomes. An outcome-free preflight at freeze (below) computes the structural upper bound of c4's breadth: c4's outgoing W2 fan-out per hidden unit is [3,2,1,0,1,1,0,0]; a reviewer's in-memory rebuild of the proposed generator found 66 of 96 genuine w1/b1 rows with structural breadth <= 1, so the upper-bound median is at most 1 and `entanglement-signal` is unreachable for c4 on any outcome. The amendment says so up front: the achievable aim of this panel is to turn `inconclusive (underpowered)` into a mechanically decided `inconclusive (neither rule met)` or `broadly-useful`, and to report A, C, K with intervals. It must not be advertised as able to show entanglement. If the preflight confirms unreachability, the go/no-go text states that a `no-go` follows from "neither rule met" and that entanglement was never testable on these genomes; testing it would need a different genome or a rule change, each a separately versioned protocol (not done here). The 0.905 etc. probabilities are illustrative, not a guarantee: they assume IID binomial edits, whereas the draw is stratified without replacement with shared loci across magnitudes and multi-edit constituents (PR136 PLAN 2.5 noted these dependencies); the amendment labels them as such and also reports P(A >= 20) and the K threshold.
3. **Fixed N, no optional stopping:** the assayed set is chosen and committed before any new assay (freeze file below); the run assays exactly that set once. Failed or crashed runs are rerun from scratch with the same frozen set, never extended or cherry-picked.
4. **Larger neighbourhood, same families.** PR136's 94-edit neighbourhood has 8 to 10 genuine edits per small stratum, so N = 192 needs bigger pools. A new panel protocol (`panel: "power-v1"`) widens only the draw counts of the same families: single-locus slots per kind `w1 40, b1 8, w2 32, b2 8` (b kinds exhaust their pools) plus the 3 physics slots, both magnitudes, sign by counter draw; `drawsPerMulti` 64 for multi2 and multi4; the equal-slot-count secondary family is not emitted in the panel (it never entered the rule). Proposed allocation per M3 genome (sum 192): single6 and single24 each `w1 22, w2 22, b1 6, b2 6, physics 1` (57 each); multi2 39; multi4 39. Population per stratum is a function of genome and operator only, so it is counted at freeze time (no outcomes) and the allocation is adjusted there only if a pool is short, by a rule written in the amendment (deficit moved to w1 of the same family). Inverse-probability weights use the real eligible population/chosen per stratum as before. **Estimand change, declared (review P2):** the multi-locus share of the assayed set rises from 6/24 to 78/192 and physics falls from 2/24 to 2/192, and both PR136 improvements were multi2 edits, so this allocation is outcome-informed. The verdict uses unweighted A/C/K and multi-vs-single counts and the breadth median includes unassayed edits, so the same numeric thresholds apply to a different mixture. The AMENDMENT states this plainly as "the same rule read on a larger, differently mixed, fresh panel", calls the result a statement about this panel's eligible neighbourhood (not about PR136's), and reports weighted estimates alongside. To limit outcome-informed tilting the allocation is fixed by a stated structural rule (equal share per family, within family proportional to pool size), written before the preflight, not tuned on PR136 improvement counts; the proposed numbers above are replaced by that rule's output at freeze.
5. **New disjoint seeds.** Assay seeds `[101, 102]` (PR136: `[1, 2]`), same count so the `3 x seeds` term of tau keeps its meaning. Neighbourhood/selection salt `0x1c0de06` (PR136: `0x1c0de05`) and null-replicate tags prefixed `power:`. Edits whose genome hash occurs anywhere in PR136's 94-edit neighbourhoods of the same start genome are excluded from the draw, so the 192 are fresh edits as well as fresh seeds. **Eligibility (review P2):** excluded edits have zero inclusion probability, so the evidence records both `generated` (full, includes excluded rows, used for breadth and descriptors) and `eligible` (generated minus PR136 hashes) populations per stratum; selection, weighting and the population recomputation in `--check` all use the same eligibility predicate (the checker is extended to take it; PR136 passes the empty predicate). A reviewer's rebuild found c4 eligible pools of 4 for single6/b1, 4 for single24/b2 and 0 for single24/physics against requested 6, 6 and 1: the frozen deficit rule (move to w1 of the same family; physics deficit goes to w1 of single24) is applied and the final redistributed allocation is what is frozen. The page and RESULT state that estimates describe the eligible complement. A test proves: assay seeds disjoint from PR136's, salt different, no assayed hash shared with PR136's assayed or unassayed rows (read from PR136 `evidence.json`).
6. **Read on its own.** PR136's 24 edits are not pooled: different seeds, hence different baselines, tau and gate, and a different neighbourhood. The page and RESULT.md show the two panels side by side only. No cross-panel statistic enters the decision.
7. **Gate and tau under new seeds** are computed from baselines only (the same code), at freeze time, and written to `freeze.json` before the run. They are baseline facts, not mutant outcomes. If a genome fails there, the AMENDMENT records the consequence mechanically; no genome swap.

## 3. What is built (one PR, reviewable size)

1. **Evidence fixture with a negative case (reuse, extended to the new seeds).** The PR136 fixtures are re-run unchanged under the new seeds: hidden-unit permutation (same function, many bytes differ: fools "bytes changed"), dead-hidden edit, clamp edit, sensor-label shuffle (a different controller, not ecological), generalist localised edit. New negative case specific to power: a **synthetic no-signal panel** test in the library (hand-built outcome table with A = 0 and with A at 4, 5 and 6) proving `decide` returns `inconclusive` for A < 5 on either genome and that a large N with the same A fraction does not by itself change a verdict (a naive "improving rate is low so it is a null" metric would call A = 4 of 192 a no-go; the rule says inconclusive). Synthetic verdict fixtures are built from consistent row-level outcomes through `buildPayload`/`readoutFor`, never by supplying `underpowered` independently (`decide()` trusts that flag). Positive and boundary fixtures through the row-to-verdict path for both genomes: `broadly-useful` at K = ceil(10% N) and one below; `entanglement-signal` with breadth at the threshold and one below; the A = 5 boundary. The earlier phrase "same A fraction at larger N does not change a verdict" is dropped: crossing `minImproving` changes the branch, and an A = 5, K = 5 input is `broadly-useful` at N = 24 but `inconclusive` at N = 192, which a test pins. And **selection-disjointness fixtures** with independent negatives (review P2): a panel with overlapping seeds only, one with unchanged salt only, and one with a single overlapping genome hash only must each fail the disjointness check for its own reason (a combined check cannot be satisfied by the seed branch alone). **Inventory fixtures:** `recomputeProblems`/verdict construction currently accept an empty neutral-fixture set through `.every()`; the power checker requires the exact expected fixture inventory and nonempty expected sample sizes, and a test removes all fixtures and expects failure (PR136's behaviour is unchanged: the shared helper takes an expected-inventory argument that PR136 sets to its current behaviour).
2. **Panel parameterisation of the existing library (behaviour-preserving).** Thread a `Panel` record `{ id, salt, seeds, singleSlots, drawsPerMulti, secondary, allocM3, allocControl, excludeHashes, candidates: { sparse, dense }, nullNamespace }` through `buildNeighbourhood`, `drawAssaySubset`, `nullBreadths()` (which today hard-codes `${id}:null:${r}` and rebuilds neighbourhoods) and the candidate selection in `runStudy`, `AssayCache/responses/tau`, `prepare`, `runStudy`, `protocolRecord`, with `PANEL_V1` = today's constants as the default. PR136 `--replay-check` must stay byte-identical (gate). Because `tools/locality/matrix.ts --check` hashes the locality sources into `manifest.json`, editing them changes those hashes; the plan re-pins only `manifest.sources` for PR136 after the replay proves `evidence.json` is byte-identical, recorded as an explicit note in the AMENDMENT. `evidence.json` and its seal are not touched.
3. **Worker pool** in `tools/locality/pool.ts` (Deno workers; none exists, PR136 cut it). PR136 measured ~75 s serial for 24+24+8 edits, so N = 192 is roughly 8x the mutant assays, a few to ~10 minutes serial; the first gate is a baseline-only serial benchmark, and the pool is built only if serial projected time exceeds the budget (serial `--workers 1` remains the reference path either way). Tasks are (genome, edit index) assays; results are collected by index and merged in fixed order so the payload is independent of worker count; `--workers` default 2 (vitest `--maxWorkers=2` does not cap nested Deno workers; verification runs are sequential, not concurrent, and budgeted in the gates). `--replay-check` equality is asserted for 1 vs 2 workers on a small subset in a test, and for the full run in the acceptance gates.
4. **Freeze step:** `tools/locality/power.ts --freeze` computes, without any mutant assay, the panel neighbourhoods, populations, final allocation, chosen edit ids and genome hashes, generalist and M3 baselines, gate/tau, and projected runtime from timing of baseline runs. Writes `freeze.json` (sealed). Committed and pushed before the run (freeze-before-run exception).
5. **Run step:** `tools/locality/power.ts --write` refuses if `freeze.json` is missing, its seal fails, or `git` shows the freeze inputs or the power source closure (hashed in `freeze.json`) differ from `HEAD`/the pushed branch (the freeze commit is resolved externally; `freeze.json` carries source and input hashes, never its own commit hash); assays exactly the frozen edits; writes `evidence.json` + `manifest.json` (payload shape of PR136 plus `panel` and `freezeSha256`); `--check` (no simulation: seal, freeze match, recomputation of every derived field and the verdict) and `--replay-check` (regenerate and require byte equality). Reuses `checkSaved`/`recomputeProblems` by parameterising paths and protocol.
6. **Verdict and RESULT.md:** `decide()` unchanged on the new payload, applied mechanically; RESULT.md (exploratory, bounded) gives A, C, K per genome, the verdict, go/no-go, deviations, side-by-side PR136 table.
7. **Page:** extend `apps/lab/controller-locality/` with a panel selector (Original, Power-up) and a side-by-side summary table (N, A, C, K, verdict, seeds, tau); same matrix view for either panel; filter by stratum remains. Local, off the deployed site (packaging test unchanged and still passing). The power panel has about 310 generated rows per genome (2x(40+8+32+8+3) singles + 2x64 multis), not 1000. The page statically imports full evidence today, so filtering does not shrink the transfer: measure the real payload first and add a generated projection only if unreasonable (then verify summaries against full evidence). `render.ts` has no stratum/kind filter today; this slice adds one.

## 4. Files

Add:
- `docs/controller-locality-power-2026-11-02/{AMENDMENT.md,freeze.json,evidence.json,manifest.json,RESULT.md,REVIEW.md}` (AMENDMENT and freeze first, outcomes later)
- `packages/lineage/src/locality/panel.ts` (Panel type, `PANEL_V1`, `PANEL_POWER_V1`, disjointness helper, power-binomial helper used by the test to reproduce the amendment's probabilities in exact integer/rational arithmetic)
- `tools/locality/pool.ts`, `tools/locality/power.ts`
- `packages/lineage/test/locality-power.test.ts`, `tools/test/locality-power-evidence.test.ts`

- `packages/lineage/src/locality/{neighbourhood,scenarios,matrix,index}.ts` (thread Panel, defaults preserve behaviour)
- `tools/locality/matrix.ts` (path/protocol parameters, shared helpers; no behaviour change for PR136)

Not changed: `packages/schema`, `packages/sim-ref`, `sim-gpu`, `RULE_VERSION`, golden pins, preregistration, deploy files.

## 5. Tests (named cases)

`packages/lineage/test/locality-power.test.ts`
- `PANEL_V1 reproduces PR136 neighbourhood, draw and protocol byte for byte`
- `power panel rule thresholds, ASSAY values except seeds, tau formula and gate equal PR136's`
- `power seeds and salt are disjoint from PR136's and no assayed hash is shared with PR136 rows`
- `disjointness test fails for a panel built with PR136's seeds` (negative fixture)
- `every stratum population covers its allocation for c4 and c9 (or the frozen deficit rule applies)`
- `allocation sums to 192 per M3 genome and 8 for the control`
- `power computation: P(A>=5) at p = 1/24 for N = 192 equals the amendment's 0.905 (exact rational)`
- `decide: A = 4 is inconclusive, A = 5 on both genomes is decidable, regardless of N` and `no-signal panel is inconclusive, not a null verdict`
- `fixtures stay neutral/non-neutral as before under the new seeds` (slow, Deno-free subset)

`tools/test/locality-power-evidence.test.ts` (Deno subprocess for slow parts, as PR136)
- `freeze.json seal verifies and its chosen set matches a recomputation without assays`
- `evidence --check passes; fails on: edited row, edited verdict, edited seed list, missing freeze, freeze mismatch, resealed derived field`
- `write refuses without freeze, with a tampered freeze, or if evidence exists`
- `worker pool result equals serial result on a small subset (1 vs 2 workers)`
- `--replay-check passes` (full)

`apps/lab/test`
- `page renders both panels, selector switches data, side-by-side table matches the two payload verdicts`
- `packaging: prototype still private and outside the deployed site`
- PR136 `tools/test/locality-evidence.test.ts` and `packages/lineage/test/locality.test.ts` remain green unchanged.

## 6. Order of work and acceptance gates

1. Panel refactor; gate: `deno run -A --no-lock tools/locality/matrix.ts --replay-check` ok (PR136 byte-identical), then re-pin manifest sources and `--check` ok.
2. Write AMENDMENT.md first, containing both exact allocations (N = 192 and the fallback N = 144, each with its deficit-rule output and stated probabilities), the timing workload and the selection rule. Timing is measured on a baseline-only workload (start-genome baseline assays per genome under both seed pairs; `power.ts --calibrate` has no code path that assays a mutant, and a test asserts that): median of 3 serial runs times the per-edit multiplier. Rule: N = 192 if projected <= 20 min loaded, else N = 144 if projected <= 20 min, else stop and report without running (no third N). Timing is outcome-free, so choosing N from it is allowed; the choice is recorded in `freeze.json` and the amendment is dated before the freeze commit.
3. Run `power.ts --freeze` (preflights included: pool sizes, c4/c9 breadth reachability, gate/tau); Sol xhigh review of the amendment/freeze; fixes; commit only AMENDMENT.md, PLAN.md, freeze.json, the freeze code and tests, push `claude/i05-power`.
4. Run once: `deno run -A --no-lock tools/locality/power.ts --write --workers 2`, then `--check` and `--replay-check`; mechanical verdict; RESULT.md.
5. Page and tests; typecheck, full suite.

Runnable gates:
```
export PATH=~/.nvm/versions/node/v24.2.0/bin:$PATH
pnpm install
pnpm typecheck
pnpm vitest run --maxWorkers=2 packages/lineage/test/locality.test.ts packages/lineage/test/locality-power.test.ts tools/test/locality-evidence.test.ts tools/test/locality-power-evidence.test.ts apps/lab/test
pnpm test --maxWorkers=2
deno run -A --no-lock tools/locality/matrix.ts --check && deno run -A --no-lock tools/locality/matrix.ts --replay-check
deno run -A --no-lock tools/locality/power.ts --check && deno run -A --no-lock tools/locality/power.ts --replay-check --workers 1 && deno run -A --no-lock tools/locality/power.ts --replay-check --workers 2   # subprocess deadlines 2x projected run; vitest wrappers use explicit timeouts, not the 60 s default or PR136's 600 s
git diff --exit-code e56077bd -- packages/schema packages/sim-ref packages/sim-gpu experiments/preregistration.md experiments/FROZEN docs/rules.md   # must exit 0 (PR136's protected set)
pnpm vitest run packages/sim-ref/test/golden-hashes.test.ts   # pins untouched
```
Then page check: `pnpm dev`, open `/controller-locality/`, both panels, screenshot at 375 and 1280 px.

## 7. Non-goals and limits

- No modular controller, no search/evolution run, no new scenarios, no new mutation operator, no change to the decision rule, tau, gate or thresholds, no GPU, no paid calls, no deploy, no change to the frozen preregistration.
- Not pooled with PR136; no claim about evolved creatures in general. Biomass responses are not independent capabilities (as in PR136). Short horizon (200), tiny tile, two seeds.
- N = 192 is a power choice under the observed rate; a low true rate (about 2%) makes a second inconclusive likely (P(A >= 5) 0.34), and that is reported as such. Even with A >= 5, the go/no-go for a fork is advisory only; any controller-layout change needs its own versioned plan, migration and CPU/WGSL parity.
- Wall time depends on machine load; timing is provenance, not part of the deterministic payload.
- Entanglement on c4 is structurally unreachable (see 2.2); a RESULT of `inconclusive` is not evidence about entanglement either way.
- Optional-stopping, extra runs after seeing A, or re-drawing the assayed set are prohibited; a reviewer-found defect before the run restarts from the freeze commit with a new dated amendment.

## 8. Revision note

Revised after the Sol 6.1 xhigh plan review (raw: `/tmp/controller-locality-power-2026-11-02-plan-review.out.txt`, final message `/tmp/i05p/plan-review-final.txt`). Dispositions in `REVIEW.md`, "Plan review dispositions".
# Amendment 1 (2026-11-02): I05b power-up of the controller-locality assay

Status: written and committed BEFORE any edited genome of this panel was assayed, and before `freeze.json` existed (the freeze binds the SHA-256 of this file). Nothing below was chosen after seeing a mutant outcome of the new panel; the only outcome-like numbers used are PR136's, which are already public (`docs/controller-locality-2026-11-01/`). Exploratory, CPU only, no GPU evidence. Local, off the deployed site.

Scope as given by the coordinator (task text; decision D12 is in `docs/backlog-run-2026-11-01.html`, D14 is quoted from the task text and is not in that file): I05 (PR136) ended inconclusive (underpowered): only 1 of 24 assayed edits improved a response for each M3 genome, against the pre-stated minimum of 5 improving edits (`RULE.minImproving`), and its EVIDENCE.md says assayed edits stayed at 24 per genome though runtime allowed more. This slice re-asks the same question with enough assayed edits for the unchanged rule to be able to decide.

## 1. What does not change

- Genomes: M3 cluster 4 (sparse slot) and M3 cluster 9 (dense slot), the two genomes of PR136's verdict. The hand-built generalist stays a positive control only (sensitivity check, neutral fixtures, tau); it does not vote. The candidate lists are singletons (`sparse [4]`, `dense [9]`): if a genome fails the viability gate under the new seeds there is no substitution (no c10, no c16). The existing rule then applies: no qualifying genome is `measurement-limited`, one is a single-genome `inconclusive`.
- Operator: `mutateInPlace` through `editFromSlots`, magnitudes 6 and 24, matched multi-locus shapes (2 x 12, 4 x 6).
- Neighbourhood families: single6, single24, multi2, multi4 (the equal-slot-count secondary family never entered the rule and is not emitted in this panel).
- Scenarios, `ASSAY` values (tile 16, radius 3, horizon 200, founder, scenario parameters, gate 1000 / 250, `viableFloor` 500) except `seeds`; tau formula; `RULE` in full (deep-equality tests against PR136's recorded `protocol.rule` and the code constant). No threshold is moved.
- No physics, schema, golden or `RULE_VERSION` change. Protected paths are checked with `git diff --exit-code e56077bd`.

## 2. Fixed N, no optional stopping

**N = 192 assayed genuine non-duplicate edits per M3 genome** (8 for the generalist, as in PR136). The set is drawn and committed before the run (`freeze.json`); the run assays exactly that set once. A crashed or failed run is rerun from scratch with the same frozen set. The set is never extended, reduced, re-drawn or cherry-picked after any outcome; a defect found before the run restarts from the freeze commit with a new dated amendment.

### 2.1 Why 192: a power argument from PR136's observed rate

PR136 observed A / N = 1/24 (4.2 percent) improving edits per genome. Illustrative model: A ~ Binomial(N, p) per genome, independent genomes. The model is illustrative, not a guarantee: the real draw is stratified without replacement, loci recur across the two magnitudes, and multi-locus edits share constituents (PR136 PLAN 2.5 noted these dependencies). Exact rational values (reproduced by `binomialAtLeast` in `packages/lineage/src/locality/panel.ts` and pinned in `packages/lineage/test/locality-power.test.ts`):

| true improving rate p | P(A >= 5), N = 192, one genome | both genomes | N = 144, one genome | both | N = 24 (PR136), one genome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 6 percent | 0.990 | 0.982 | 0.937 | 0.878 | 0.012 |
| 1/24 (PR136) | **0.905** | **0.819** | 0.720 | 0.519 | 0.0027 |
| 3.5 percent | 0.804 | 0.647 | 0.569 | 0.324 | 0.0013 |
| 3 percent | 0.685 | 0.469 | 0.434 | 0.188 | 0.0006 |
| 2 percent | 0.339 | 0.115 | 0.163 | 0.026 | 0.0001 |

At the rate PR136 saw, the probability that A >= 5 on both genomes (so that the rule can leave its underpowered branch) is **0.82 at N = 192**, and under 0.01 at N = 24. N = 240 would give about 0.95 but is not affordable in the budget below. Power below p of about 3 percent is low: a second `inconclusive (underpowered)` is a legitimate outcome and is reported as such.

### 2.2 CPU budget and the deterministic choice of N

Budget: about 20 minutes on a loaded machine. Rule, applied to a baseline-only timing and fixed in code (`chooseN` in `tools/locality/power.ts`): N = 192 if the projected total is at most 1200 s, else N = 144 if that is, else stop and report without running (no third size). The timing workload is `power.ts --calibrate`: the start-genome baseline assays of the generalist, M3 c4 and M3 c9 under both seed pairs (36 runs) and one probe of each, median of 3 serial repetitions; it has no code path that assays an edited genome (a test asserts `mutantAssays` is 0 and the run count is 36). Projection = counted work times the median per-run and per-probe cost, where the counted work is 6 runs per assayed edit, the baselines, 100 runs for fixtures and slack, and every probe (each generated edit once, plus the null replicates).

Measured while writing this amendment on a machine at load average 20 to 30: about 0.13 s per run, 0.009 s per probe. Projection: **about 340 s at N = 192 and 270 s at N = 144 (freeze.json records its own re-measurement)**, both well inside 1200 s, so N = 192 is chosen. `freeze.json` re-measures and records the same numbers; the choice follows by the rule. Timing is provenance, not part of any deterministic payload.

**The worker pool of PLAN 3.3 is not built**: its own condition ("only if the serial projection exceeds the budget") is not met. `--workers n` is accepted, recorded in the manifest as requested 1 or 2 and used 1, and has no effect; serial is the reference path. The PLAN's 1-versus-2-workers equality test therefore does not exist.

### 2.3 What A >= 5 does and does not buy

`decide()` is unchanged. A >= 5 on both genomes moves the rule out of the `underpowered` guard into `broadly-useful`, `entanglement-signal`, or the residual `inconclusive` (the genomes disagree or neither rule is met). That residual `inconclusive` is a decided outcome of the rule, distinct from the underpowered one, and is reported as such.

- `broadly-useful` additionally needs K >= 10 percent of assayed genuine edits, i.e. K >= 20 at N = 192 (K >= 15 at 144), and K >= half of A, and a multi-locus clean share within 5 points of the single-locus one. Under the binomial model at p = 1/24, P(A >= 20) is 0.0002 at N = 192 (P(A >= 15) is 0.0011 at N = 144). A `broadly-useful` call is very unlikely unless the true improving rate is several times PR136's.
- `entanglement-signal` needs, on both genomes, collateral C >= half of A, median breadth (over all generated genuine single-locus w1/b1 edits, doubled units) >= 6 (3 actuators) and >= null + 2. An outcome-free preflight at freeze (`breadthUpperBound2` in `freeze.json`: the median of the wiring bound popcount of structurally reachable actuators over those edits, which bounds the realised breadth from above) gives **2 for M3 c4**: c4 cannot reach the breadth threshold on any outcome, so `entanglement-signal` is **unreachable on this pair**. For c9 the bound is 16 (reachable by the bound). This panel therefore cannot show entanglement and must not be advertised as able to; the achievable aim is to turn `inconclusive (underpowered)` into a mechanically decided `inconclusive (neither rule met)` or `broadly-useful`, and to report A, C, K with the finite-panel counts. Testing entanglement would need a different genome or a rule change, each a separately versioned protocol (not done here).

### 2.4 Pre-stated reading of the result for the go/no-go

- `broadly-useful`: no-go for an architecture fork; better search and incentives first (the page's own wording).
- `entanglement-signal`: advisory go for a separate, separately versioned study of a representation fork (unreachable on c4, so not expected).
- `inconclusive` (any branch), `measurement-limited`: no-go on this evidence; not a refutation. If still underpowered (A < 5 on a genome) RESULT.md says so and does not turn the low rate into a null.
- In every case exploratory and bounded to these two genomes, the one-step operator, the 26,244-point probe grid and the three 16x16 tiny-world biomass assays; any controller-layout change needs its own versioned plan, migration and CPU/WGSL parity.

## 3. The larger neighbourhood and the allocation rule

New panel `power-v1` widens only the draw counts of the same families: single-locus slots per kind `w1 40, b1 8, w2 32, b2 8` (the b kinds exhaust their pools) plus the 3 physics slots, both magnitudes, sign by counter draw; `drawsPerMulti` 64 for multi2 and for multi4.

**Allocation rule (a structural rule written before any pool was counted; not tuned on PR136 improvement counts).** Equal share N/4 for each family (single6, single24, multi2, multi4). Within a single-locus family the share is split over w1, b1, w2, b2, physics proportionally to the ELIGIBLE pool sizes by largest remainder (ties by that order). A kind is capped at its pool and the deficit moves to w1 of the same family, then w2, b1, b2, physics while capacity remains. A multi family takes min(share, pool); its deficit moves to the other multi family, then to w1 of single24, then of single6. If the total cannot be placed the freeze stops. The allocation is recomputed from the saved rows by `--check` and must equal the frozen one.

Pools (genuine non-duplicate edits per stratum; "eligible" excludes edits whose genome hash occurs among PR136's edits of the same start genome, see section 4) and the rule's output at both sizes:

| stratum | c4 generated | c4 eligible | c4 N=192 | c4 N=144 | c9 generated | c9 eligible | c9 N=192 | c9 N=144 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| single6/w1 | 40 | 36 | 22 | 16 | 40 | 37 | 23 | 17 |
| single6/b1 | 8 | 4 | 2 | 2 | 8 | 6 | 4 | 3 |
| single6/w2 | 32 | 31 | 19 | 14 | 32 | 28 | 17 | 13 |
| single6/b2 | 8 | 7 | 4 | 3 | 8 | 4 | 3 | 2 |
| single6/physics | 2 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 3 | 2 | 1 | 1 |
| single24/w1 | 40 | 36 | 22 | 17 | 40 | 36 | 23 | 17 |
| single24/b1 | 8 | 6 | 4 | 3 | 8 | 7 | 4 | 3 |
| single24/w2 | 32 | 31 | 19 | 14 | 32 | 26 | 17 | 13 |
| single24/b2 | 8 | 4 | 3 | 2 | 8 | 4 | 3 | 2 |
| single24/physics | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 3 | 2 | 1 | 1 |
| multi2/multi | 63 | 63 | 48 | 36 | 64 | 64 | 48 | 36 |
| multi4/multi | 58 | 58 | 48 | 36 | 64 | 64 | 48 | 36 |

The generalist (control, 8 edits: single24 w1 2, w2 2, b1 1, b2 1, physics 1, multi2 1) has enough eligible edits in every stratum except single24/physics (eligible 1, needs 1), which is enough.

The frozen allocations (checked against `freeze.json` by a test):


**Estimand change, declared.** The multi-locus share of the assayed set is 96/192 (PR136: 6/24 per genome) and physics edits fall to 1 or 2 of 192 (PR136: 2/24); both PR136 improvements were multi2 edits, so a larger multi share could raise A. The allocation follows the structural rule above, not those counts, but the effect exists. The verdict uses unweighted A, C, K and the unweighted multi-versus-single clean shares, and the breadth median includes unassayed edits, so the same numeric thresholds apply to a different mixture. This is "the same rule read on a larger, differently mixed, fresh panel"; the result is a statement about this panel's eligible neighbourhood, not about PR136's. Inverse-probability-weighted estimates (eligible population over chosen, per stratum) are reported alongside.

## 4. New disjoint seeds, fresh edits, eligibility

- Assay seeds `[101, 102]` (PR136: `[1, 2]`); same count, so the `3 x seeds` term of tau and the viable floor keep their meaning.
- Neighbourhood and selection salt `0x1c0de06` (PR136: `0x1c0de05`); degree-matched null replicate tags prefixed `power:` (`power:<genome>:null:<r>`).
- Every edit whose genome hash occurs anywhere in PR136's 94-edit neighbourhood of the same start genome (assayed or not) is excluded from the draw: zero inclusion probability. The evidence records `generated` (full, includes the excluded rows; used for breadth and descriptors) and `eligible` per stratum; selection, weighting and the population recomputation in `--check` use the same predicate; the exclusion lists are stored verbatim in the panel record, so `--check` does not need PR136 to recompute them (it compares them to PR136's saved hashes when present). Estimates describe the eligible complement.
- Tests: seeds disjoint, salt different, null namespace different, no assayed hash shared with any PR136 row; independent negative fixtures (overlapping seeds only, equal salt only, equal namespace only, one overlapping hash only, each failing for its own reason).
- Baselines, gate and tau under the new seeds are computed from baselines only (the same code) at freeze and written to `freeze.json`. They are baseline facts, not mutant outcomes.

## 5. The new panel is read on its own

PR136's 24 edits are NOT pooled: different seeds hence different baselines, tau and gate, and a different neighbourhood. No cross-panel statistic enters the decision. The page and RESULT.md show the two panels side by side only.

## 6. Procedure and guards

1. Library parameterisation (`Panel`; `PANEL_V1` is today's constants and PR136 `--replay-check` stays byte-identical, gate passed before this amendment: 59 s under Deno). Because `tools/locality/matrix.ts --check` hashes the locality sources, adding `panel.ts` and editing the others changes those hashes: only `manifest.sources` of PR136 is re-pinned, after the replay proved `evidence.json` byte-identical. `evidence.json` and its seal are untouched.
2. `power.ts --freeze` writes `freeze.json` (sealed): N, allocations at both sizes, panel record with exclusion lists, baselines, gate, tau, chosen edit ids and hashes, fixture inventory, preflight, timing, SHA-256 of this file, of PR136's `evidence.json` and of the source closure (library, `genotype.ts`, `tools/locality/matrix.ts`, `tools/locality/power.ts`).
3. A Codex Sol 6.1 xhigh review of this amendment and the freeze; defects fixed; then ONLY the freeze files (this amendment, PLAN.md, REVIEW.md, `freeze.json`, the code and tests, PR136's re-pinned manifest) are committed and pushed to `claude/i05-power`. No outcome file is in that commit.
4. `power.ts --write` refuses if `freeze.json` is missing, its seal fails, any frozen input or source differs from its frozen hash, any of them differs from `HEAD`, the freeze commit is not an ancestor of `origin/claude/i05-power`, or `evidence.json` exists. Before the first edited genome is assayed it rebuilds the neighbourhoods and asserts that the drawn ids and hashes equal the frozen set. It writes `evidence.json` and `manifest.json` once.
5. `--check` (no simulation: seal, freeze match, exact fixture inventory, recomputation of every derived field, the allocation from the eligible pools, disjointness, the verdict) and `--replay-check` (regenerate and require byte equality). The verdict is `decide()` on the saved rows, applied mechanically; RESULT.md reports A, C, K, the verdict, the go/no-go of section 2.4, deviations.
6. Page: `/controller-locality/` gets a panel selector and a side-by-side table. No physics, schema or golden change; no paid calls; no deploy.

## 7. Limits

Biomass responses are not independent capabilities (as in PR136). Short horizon (200), tiny tile, two seeds, one-step operator, two genomes. Not a claim about evolved creatures in general. Even with A >= 5 the go/no-go is advisory.

# Review log: I05b controller locality power-up

## Plan review dispositions

Reviewer: Codex Sol 6.1 xhigh, read-only. Raw output: `/tmp/controller-locality-power-2026-11-02-plan-review.out.txt`; final message: `/tmp/i05p/plan-review-final.txt`. Prompt: `/tmp/controller-locality-power-2026-11-02-plan-review.md`. PLAN.md revised accordingly. Review checked that the binomial probabilities in the plan are arithmetically correct, that the named APIs exist, and that re-pinning PR136 `manifest.sources` does not break its seal.

| # | Sev | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | P1 | `entanglement-signal` unreachable for c4 (structural breadth <= 1 for most w1/b1 edits; rule needs median >= 3 actuators on both genomes) | Accepted. PLAN 2.2 adds an outcome-free freeze preflight and states up front that entanglement is untestable on c4; the go/no-go says so. Genome swap and rule change stay out of scope (separately versioned). |
| 2 | P1 | Power target A >= 5 ignores K >= 10% of assayed (K >= 20 at N = 192; P about 0.0002); "same A fraction" test claim false | Accepted. PLAN 2.2 reports reachable verdicts and P(A >= 20); aim restated as turning underpowered into a mechanically decided result; tests reworked with positive/boundary fixtures; false claim removed and replaced by an N = 24 vs 192 pin. |
| 3 | P2 | Allocation (multi 78/192, physics 2/192) changes the estimand and is outcome-informed; IID binomial is illustrative | Accepted. Declared in PLAN 2.4 and 2.2; allocation by a stated structural rule written before preflight; probabilities labelled illustrative; weighted estimates reported alongside. |
| 4 | P2 | PR136 hash exclusion conflicts with the population checker; some pools are short (c4: 4, 4, 0) | Accepted. Generated vs eligible populations, shared eligibility predicate, deficit rule frozen with final allocation (PLAN 2.5). |
| 5 | P2 | Panel omits candidate lists and null namespace; `nullBreadths()` hard-codes tags | Accepted. Panel gains `candidates` (singleton c4, c9) and `nullNamespace`, threaded through `nullBreadths()` and `runStudy`; tests for no-substitution gate failure and actual null seeds (PLAN 3.2, 2.1). |
| 6 | P2 | Negative fixtures can be vacuous; empty neutral-fixture set passes; `decide()` trusts `underpowered` | Accepted. Independent negatives for seed/salt/hash overlap; exact fixture inventory in the power checker; verdict fixtures built from consistent rows (PLAN 5 / 3.1). |
| 7 | P2 | 144 fallback and timing gate not frozen; calibration before amendment | Accepted. Amendment first with both allocations, baseline-only timing workload with a test that no mutant is assayed, deterministic N rule, stop condition (PLAN 6.2). |
| 8 | P2 | Source hashing omits `power.ts`/`pool.ts`; self-referential commit hash | Accepted. Own source closure incl. runner and worker; freeze binds to input/source hashes, commit resolved externally (PLAN 3.2, 3.5). |
| 9 | P2 | Worker-count equality not in gates; test deadlines (60 s / 600 s) vs 20 min run | Accepted. Explicit `--workers 1` and `2` replay gates, explicit deadlines; pool built only if serial projection exceeds budget (PLAN 3.3, 6). |
| 10 | P2 | Protected-file gate does not fail on diffs; protected set incomplete | Accepted. `git diff --exit-code e56077bd` over schema, sim-ref, sim-gpu, preregistration, FROZEN, rules.md. |
| 11 | P3 | Page row count wrong (310 not 1000); static import; no stratum filter exists | Accepted (PLAN 3.7). Measure first; add filter explicitly. |
| 12 | P3 | D14 absent from backlog log (ends at D13) | Accepted. PLAN header cites only D12 from the file and says D14 comes from the coordinator's task text. |

Not changed: the review judged baseline-only tau recomputation, all-hash exclusion and not pooling defensible.

 export const candidatesText = (p: Payload): string => p.candidates.map((c) => `cluster ${c.cluster} (${c.slot}): ${c.qualification.pass ? 'passed the viability gate' : `failed: ${c.qualification.reasons.join('; ')}`}${c.chosen ? ', used' : ''}`).join(' | ');
+
+export interface NamedPanel { id: string; label: string; p: Payload }
+
+/** Side-by-side counts of the panels: one row per genome per panel, never pooled. Each panel is read on its own. */
+export function panelSummaryTable(panels: readonly NamedPanel[]): string {
+  const head = '<thead><tr><th scope="col">Panel</th><th scope="col">Controller</th><th scope="col">Assay seeds</th><th scope="col">Assayed edits (N)</th><th scope="col">Improve one response (A)</th><th scope="col">Of those, harm another (C)</th><th scope="col">Of those, harm none (K)</th><th scope="col">Weighted improving rate</th><th scope="col">Tolerance (light / dark / dim)</th><th scope="col">Rule outcome</th></tr></thead>';
+  const rows = panels.flatMap(({ label, p }) => p.genomes.map((g) => {
+    const r = p.readouts.find((x) => x.genome === g.id)!;
+    const t = r.overall;
+    const seeds = ((p.protocol as { assay?: { seeds?: number[] } }).assay?.seeds ?? []).join(', ');
+    const ipw = r.ipw.improvingPermille;
+    const role = g.votes ? '' : g.role === 'control' ? ' (positive control, does not vote)' : ' (did not qualify)';
+    return `<tr><th scope="row">${esc(label)}</th><td>${esc(g.label)}${esc(role)}</td><td>${esc(seeds)}</td><td>${t.assayedGenuine}</td><td>${pm(t.improving, t.assayedGenuine)}</td><td>${pm(t.collateral, t.improving)}</td><td>${pm(t.clean, t.improving)}</td><td>${ipw === null ? '-' : `${ipw} permille`}</td><td>${g.tau.join(' / ')}</td><td>${esc(verdictText(p.verdict))}</td></tr>`;
+  })).join('');
+  return `${head}<tbody>${rows}</tbody>`;
+}
+
+export const panelNote = (p: Payload): string => {
+  const panel = (p as { panel?: { id: string; seeds: readonly number[] } }).panel;
+  const seeds = ((p.protocol as { assay?: { seeds?: number[] } }).assay?.seeds ?? []).join(' and ');
+  const n = p.genomes.filter((g) => g.votes).map((g) => `${g.label}: ${p.rows.filter((r) => r.genome === g.id && r.assay).length}`).join('; ');
+  return `${panel ? `Power-up panel (${panel.id}), a fresh larger draw` : 'Original panel (PR136)'}: assay seeds ${seeds}; assayed edits per voting controller (${n}). Read on its own: not pooled with the other panel.`;
+};

# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

An open-ended artificial-life ecology on WebGPU: closed in matter, open in energy, exact integer physics. Individuals, reproduction and heredity are *inferred* from local rules, never declared (no `Organism` class, no `reproduce()`). Research goal and milestones (M3 bootstrap → M4/M5 ensembles): `docs/plan.md`. Rules in prose plus the arithmetic-bounds argument: `docs/rules.md`.

The repo is a pnpm workspace (TypeScript packages + Vite lab), a Deno toolchain for headless GPU runs, and a Phoenix app (`apps/coordinator`). It is jj-colocated with git (`.jj/`).

## Commands

```bash
pnpm install
pnpm dev                 # lab at http://localhost:5173 (/selftest.html, /island.html)
bin/dev                  # lab + coordinator together (PORT, LAB_PORT override 4000/5173)
pnpm typecheck           # tsc over packages/*/src, apps/lab/src, tests (excludes tests/deno)
pnpm test                # vitest: apps/lab/test, packages/*/test, experiments/test, tools/test
pnpm vitest run packages/sim-ref/test/ref.test.ts -t "<name>"   # single test
deno run -A tests/deno/gpu_golden.ts    # GPU vs CPU reference, bit for bit (native WebGPU)
npx playwright test      # browser golden (tests/e2e), Chrome with WebGPU flags; BL_ALL_ENGINES=1 adds firefox/webkit
BL_E2E_PORT=5241 npx playwright test   # a second worktree on its own port (default 5174; strictPort, never reuses a server)
cd apps/coordinator && mix test         # or `mix test test/coordinator/queue_test.exs`
cd apps/coordinator && mix precommit    # warnings-as-errors, format, test
pnpm gen:prereg          # regenerate experiments/preregistration.md generated sections
pnpm gen:cards           # redraw the link-preview cards (site + one per world) with Chrome/WebGPU; then look at them
```

Run `pnpm install` before any `deno run` in a fresh checkout (deno 2.9 otherwise writes `workspaces` into package.json). Tests that start deno pass `--no-lock` (`tools/test/deno-no-lock.test.ts` checks; a launcher or coordinator a test starts that runs deno itself gets the PATH shim in `tests/deno/lib/no_lock_deno.ts`), but a top-level `deno run`, or a tool that starts deno, still writes a stale deno.lock: when package.json deps change, update it deliberately (`deno cache --frozen tools/stitch.ts` fails while it is stale).

`tests/deno/*.ts` are standalone scripts (not `Deno.test`); run each with `deno run -A tests/deno/<name>.ts`. Several need a real GPU; `coordinator_integration.ts` spawns `mix phx.server` against a scratch data dir.

Experiment CLIs (`tools/`, all Deno, native WebGPU): `run.ts` (write run bundles to `runs/<experiment>/<preset>/<condition>/seed-<n>/`), `analyze.ts` (ensemble analysis), `bootstrap.ts` (M3 MAP-Elites search), `retest.ts`, `calibrate.ts`, `island.ts` (headless island), `stitch.ts` (coordinator segments → bundles). Usage is in each file's header comment and in README.md. `runs/` is gitignored and can hold very large outputs.

## Architecture

**Package imports.** `@bl/*` resolve to `packages/*/src/index.ts` via four separate maps that must stay in sync: `tsconfig.json` paths, `vitest.config.ts` aliases, `deno.json` imports, and `apps/lab/vite.config.ts` aliases. There is no build step for packages.

**Two implementations of one rule set.**
- `packages/sim-ref/src/step.ts` is the executable specification (CPU).
- `packages/sim-gpu/src/shaders.ts` (WGSL) must match it bit for bit. `packages/sim-gpu/src/golden.ts` defines golden cases and runs on any WebGPU host (Deno, browser `/selftest.html`, Playwright).
- `packages/sim-ref/test/golden-hashes.test.ts` pins state hashes per `RULE_VERSION` (in `packages/schema/src/config.ts`). Any change to dynamics changes the hashes: an intended rule change bumps `RULE_VERSION` and re-pins deliberately. A change that isn't meant to alter dynamics must leave every pin untouched — new optional `WorldConfig` keys stay absent from `defaultConfig()` so existing configs hash identically.
- All arithmetic is integer (u32 matter, fixed-point controllers, counter-based PRNG keyed on seed/step/cell, stochastic rounding with remainders to the source). Conservation residual is exactly zero. Keep new arithmetic inside the bounds listed in `docs/rules.md` (`MATTER_MAX`, `POOL_MAX`, `mulShr` limits); `validateConfig`/`validateState` enforce them.

**Packages.** `schema` (config, presets, layout, genome, worlds, checkpoint encoding, migration/exchange, M3 founder set) → `sim-ref`, `sim-gpu` → `metrics` (census, tracker, collectives, activity, complexity, stats; runs on readbacks) → `runner` (conditions, `runExperiment`, observers, stitching, island client) and `search` (MAP-Elites, gates). `lineage` (schema, sim-ref) is the lineage inspector's genotype core: keys, ancestry, mutation replay, the controller probe, and the mutation-edge store the lab keeps. `runner` code runs unchanged in browsers and Deno.

**Checkpoints and replay.** A checkpoint artifact carries physics state and observer state together, under one digest. Segmented runs must equal a continuous run byte for byte (`tests/deno/segments.ts`, `tests/deno/stitch.ts`); this determinism is what makes distributed verification possible.

**Archipelago.** `apps/coordinator` (Phoenix, JSON API under `/api`, static status page `priv/static/index.html`) hands islands segment tasks. `Coordinator.Queue` does scheduling, `Coordinator.Segment` owns segment state transitions, and `Coordinator.Store` is content-addressed storage for checkpoints (`.blck`) and bundle files. A fraction of segments is re-run by a second island and compared by state hash. Islands are `apps/lab/island.html` (browser) or `tools/island.ts` (Deno), both via `packages/runner/src/island.ts`. `apps/coordinator/AGENTS.md` has Phoenix/Elixir/OTP conventions to follow there; everything between its `usage-rules` markers is generated by `mix usage_rules.sync` (config in `mix.exs`), so rerun that after dependency changes rather than editing the section. `mix usage_rules.search_docs "<term>" -p <pkg>` searches hexdocs for the coordinator's deps.

**Lab.** `apps/lab/src/sim.worker.ts` owns the WebGPU device, frame loop, rendering and OPFS storage. `apps/lab/src/execution.ts` owns census cadence, observer integrity, checkpoint settlement and replay traversal. State-changing messages run through `exclusive()`, which waits for in-flight frame execution; generation identity invalidates stale readbacks.

**Pre-registration.** `experiments/endpoints.ts` is the source of truth for primary endpoints, held-out observables and activity thresholds, and `tools/analyze.ts` executes it. The generated sections of `experiments/preregistration.md` (between `GENERATED` markers) come from `pnpm gen:prereg`, and `experiments/test/prereg-sync.test.ts` fails when they are stale. Edit `endpoints.ts` and regenerate; never hand-edit inside the markers. The doc is frozen (2026-09-27): its SHA-256 is recorded in `experiments/FROZEN`, `pnpm gen:prereg` now refuses to rewrite the generated sections, and every change goes in a dated amendment section.

**Analysis scale.** Run bundles can be hundreds of MB per run (for example `lineages.tsv`). Analysis tools stream per census rather than loading whole tables; keep new analysis code streaming.

## Codex reviews

Reviews by Codex use **Sol 6.1 at High effort**, not Astra (standing instruction from 2026-10-03, replaces the earlier `gpt-6-astra` policy). Run them read-only at phase boundaries and always before committing:

```bash
codex exec -m gpt-6.1-sol -c model_reasoning_effort=high -s read-only -C <checkout> - < prompt.md > review.txt
```

Use `< /dev/null` when the prompt is an argument (otherwise codex waits on stdin) and `-o <file>` for the final message. Historical "Astra review" comments in the code are provenance for past reviews; leave them. Delegated agents and workspaces follow the same rule.

# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

An open-ended artificial-life ecology on WebGPU: closed in matter, open in energy, exact integer physics. Individuals, reproduction and heredity are *inferred* from local rules, never declared (no `Organism` class, no `reproduce()`). Research goal and milestones (M3 bootstrap → M4/M5 ensembles): `docs/plan.md`. Rules in prose plus the arithmetic-bounds argument: `docs/rules.md`.

The repo is a pnpm workspace (TypeScript packages + Vite lab), a Deno toolchain for headless GPU runs, and a Phoenix app (`apps/coordinator`). It is jj-colocated with git (`.jj/`).

## Commands

```bash
pnpm install
pnpm dev                 # lab at http://localhost:5173 (/selftest.html, /island.html)
bin/dev                  # lab + coordinator together (PORT, LAB_PORT override 4000/5173)
pnpm typecheck           # tsc over packages/*/src, apps/lab/src, tests (excludes tests/deno)
pnpm test                # vitest: apps/lab/test, packages/*/test, experiments/test, tools/test
pnpm vitest run packages/sim-ref/test/ref.test.ts -t "<name>"   # single test
deno run -A tests/deno/gpu_golden.ts    # GPU vs CPU reference, bit for bit (native WebGPU)
npx playwright test      # browser golden (tests/e2e), Chrome with WebGPU flags; BL_ALL_ENGINES=1 adds firefox/webkit
BL_E2E_PORT=5241 npx playwright test   # a second worktree on its own port (default 5174; strictPort, never reuses a server)
cd apps/coordinator && mix test         # or `mix test test/coordinator/queue_test.exs`
cd apps/coordinator && mix precommit    # warnings-as-errors, format, test
pnpm gen:prereg          # regenerate experiments/preregistration.md generated sections
pnpm gen:cards           # redraw the link-preview cards (site + one per world) with Chrome/WebGPU; then look at them
```

Run `pnpm install` before any `deno run` in a fresh checkout (deno 2.9 otherwise writes `workspaces` into package.json). Tests that start deno pass `--no-lock` (`tools/test/deno-no-lock.test.ts` checks; a launcher or coordinator a test starts that runs deno itself gets the PATH shim in `tests/deno/lib/no_lock_deno.ts`), but a top-level `deno run`, or a tool that starts deno, still writes a stale deno.lock: when package.json deps change, update it deliberately (`deno cache --frozen tools/stitch.ts` fails while it is stale).

`tests/deno/*.ts` are standalone scripts (not `Deno.test`); run each with `deno run -A tests/deno/<name>.ts`. Several need a real GPU; `coordinator_integration.ts` spawns `mix phx.server` against a scratch data dir.

# I05b Controller locality power-up: plan

Status: PLAN stage, 2026-11-02. Branch `claude/i05-power` from `origin/main` e56077bd. Source: "I05" and "Agent execution contract" in `docs/simulation-research-2026-11-01/report.md`; coordinator decision D12 in `docs/backlog-run-2026-11-01.html` (that file ends at D13 in this checkout; D14 is cited from the coordinator's task text, not found in the file, and the AMENDMENT quotes the scope as given rather than claiming a D14 clause). Builds on PR136 (`docs/controller-locality-2026-11-01/`, code in `packages/lineage/src/locality/`, `tools/locality/matrix.ts`, page `apps/lab/controller-locality/`). Exploratory, CPU only, no GPU, no physics/schema/golden change.

## 1. Bounded question

PR136 ended inconclusive (underpowered): M3 clusters 4 and 9 each had A = 1 improving edit of 24 assayed, against the pre-stated minimum of 5 (`RULE.minImproving`). Question: with enough assayed edits for the unchanged rule to be able to decide, what does it decide for the same two genomes, same operator, same neighbourhood families, same scenarios and thresholds? The slice does not ask anything new about controllers and does not change the rule. Output: a verdict from `decide()` plus a go/no-go for a separately versioned controller architecture, exploratory and bounded to these two genomes, the one-step operator, the 26,244-point probe grid and the three 16x16 tiny-world biomass assays.

## 2. Design decisions (to be fixed in AMENDMENT.md before any new assay)

1. **Unchanged:** genomes (M3 c4 sparse, M3 c9 dense; generalist is control only, does not vote), operator (`mutateInPlace` through `editFromSlots`), families (single6, single24, multi2, multi4), scenarios and `ASSAY` values except `seeds`, `tau` formula, gate (1000 / 250), `viableFloor`, `RULE` (deep-equality test against PR136's `protocolRecord().rule` and a pinned snapshot). No substitution of genomes: the panel carries singleton candidate lists (`sparse [4]`, `dense [9]`), so `runStudy` cannot fall through to c10 or try c16; if a genome fails the gate under the new seeds the verdict follows the existing rule (`measurement-limited` or single-genome `inconclusive`), pre-stated.
2. **Fixed N = 192 assayed genuine non-duplicate edits per M3 genome** (8 for the generalist, as before, only for the sensitivity and neutral controls and for tau). Power argument: PR136 observed A/N = 1/24 = 4.2% per genome. With A ~ Binomial(192, p): P(A >= 5) = 0.905 at p = 1/24; 0.805 at 3.5%; 0.685 at 3.0%; 0.340 at 2.0%; 0.991 at 6.0%. Both genomes need A >= 5: 0.82 at p = 1/24 for both (independent). N = 144 gives 0.72 per genome (0.52 both); N = 240 gives 0.97 (0.95) but is not affordable at the CPU budget. The AMENDMENT states these numbers and that power below p ~ 3% is low: a second inconclusive is a legitimate outcome and still reported. **What A >= 5 does and does not buy (review P1).** `decide()` is unchanged, so the amendment states which verdicts are reachable, with numbers: (a) A >= 5 on both genomes only moves the rule out of the `underpowered` guard, into `broadly-useful`, `entanglement-signal`, or the residual `inconclusive` (disagreement or neither rule met). That residual `inconclusive` is a decided outcome of the rule, distinct from the underpowered one, and is reported as such. (b) `broadly-useful` additionally needs K >= 10% of assayed genuine edits, i.e. K >= 20 at N = 192 (K >= 15 at 144); under the binomial model at p = 1/24 P(A >= 20) is about 0.0002 (0.0011 at N = 144), so a `broadly-useful` call is very unlikely unless the true improving rate is several times PR136's. (c) `entanglement-signal` needs median breadth (over all generated genuine edits, doubled units) >= 6 doubled (3 actuators) and >= null + 2 on both genomes. An outcome-free preflight at freeze (below) computes the structural upper bound of c4's breadth: c4's outgoing W2 fan-out per hidden unit is [3,2,1,0,1,1,0,0]; a reviewer's in-memory rebuild of the proposed generator found 66 of 96 genuine w1/b1 rows with structural breadth <= 1, so the upper-bound median is at most 1 and `entanglement-signal` is unreachable for c4 on any outcome. The amendment says so up front: the achievable aim of this panel is to turn `inconclusive (underpowered)` into a mechanically decided `inconclusive (neither rule met)` or `broadly-useful`, and to report A, C, K with intervals. It must not be advertised as able to show entanglement. If the preflight confirms unreachability, the go/no-go text states that a `no-go` follows from "neither rule met" and that entanglement was never testable on these genomes; testing it would need a different genome or a rule change, each a separately versioned protocol (not done here). The 0.905 etc. probabilities are illustrative, not a guarantee: they assume IID binomial edits, whereas the draw is stratified without replacement with shared loci across magnitudes and multi-edit constituents (PR136 PLAN 2.5 noted these dependencies); the amendment labels them as such and also reports P(A >= 20) and the K threshold.
3. **Fixed N, no optional stopping:** the assayed set is chosen and committed before any new assay (freeze file below); the run assays exactly that set once. Failed or crashed runs are rerun from scratch with the same frozen set, never extended or cherry-picked.
4. **Larger neighbourhood, same families.** PR136's 94-edit neighbourhood has 8 to 10 genuine edits per small stratum, so N = 192 needs bigger pools. A new panel protocol (`panel: "power-v1"`) widens only the draw counts of the same families: single-locus slots per kind `w1 40, b1 8, w2 32, b2 8` (b kinds exhaust their pools) plus the 3 physics slots, both magnitudes, sign by counter draw; `drawsPerMulti` 64 for multi2 and multi4; the equal-slot-count secondary family is not emitted in the panel (it never entered the rule). Proposed allocation per M3 genome (sum 192): single6 and single24 each `w1 22, w2 22, b1 6, b2 6, physics 1` (57 each); multi2 39; multi4 39. Population per stratum is a function of genome and operator only, so it is counted at freeze time (no outcomes) and the allocation is adjusted there only if a pool is short, by a rule written in the amendment (deficit moved to w1 of the same family). Inverse-probability weights use the real eligible population/chosen per stratum as before. **Estimand change, declared (review P2):** the multi-locus share of the assayed set rises from 6/24 to 78/192 and physics falls from 2/24 to 2/192, and both PR136 improvements were multi2 edits, so this allocation is outcome-informed. The verdict uses unweighted A/C/K and multi-vs-single counts and the breadth median includes unassayed edits, so the same numeric thresholds apply to a different mixture. The AMENDMENT states this plainly as "the same rule read on a larger, differently mixed, fresh panel", calls the result a statement about this panel's eligible neighbourhood (not about PR136's), and reports weighted estimates alongside. To limit outcome-informed tilting the allocation is fixed by a stated structural rule (equal share per family, within family proportional to pool size), written before the preflight, not tuned on PR136 improvement counts; the proposed numbers above are replaced by that rule's output at freeze.
5. **New disjoint seeds.** Assay seeds `[101, 102]` (PR136: `[1, 2]`), same count so the `3 x seeds` term of tau keeps its meaning. Neighbourhood/selection salt `0x1c0de06` (PR136: `0x1c0de05`) and null-replicate tags prefixed `power:`. Edits whose genome hash occurs anywhere in PR136's 94-edit neighbourhoods of the same start genome are excluded from the draw, so the 192 are fresh edits as well as fresh seeds. **Eligibility (review P2):** excluded edits have zero inclusion probability, so the evidence records both `generated` (full, includes excluded rows, used for breadth and descriptors) and `eligible` (generated minus PR136 hashes) populations per stratum; selection, weighting and the population recomputation in `--check` all use the same eligibility predicate (the checker is extended to take it; PR136 passes the empty predicate). A reviewer's rebuild found c4 eligible pools of 4 for single6/b1, 4 for single24/b2 and 0 for single24/physics against requested 6, 6 and 1: the frozen deficit rule (move to w1 of the same family; physics deficit goes to w1 of single24) is applied and the final redistributed allocation is what is frozen. The page and RESULT state that estimates describe the eligible complement. A test proves: assay seeds disjoint from PR136's, salt different, no assayed hash shared with PR136's assayed or unassayed rows (read from PR136 `evidence.json`).
6. **Read on its own.** PR136's 24 edits are not pooled: different seeds, hence different baselines, tau and gate, and a different neighbourhood. The page and RESULT.md show the two panels side by side only. No cross-panel statistic enters the decision.
7. **Gate and tau under new seeds** are computed from baselines only (the same code), at freeze time, and written to `freeze.json` before the run. They are baseline facts, not mutant outcomes. If a genome fails there, the AMENDMENT records the consequence mechanically; no genome swap.

## 3. What is built (one PR, reviewable size)

1. **Evidence fixture with a negative case (reuse, extended to the new seeds).** The PR136 fixtures are re-run unchanged under the new seeds: hidden-unit permutation (same function, many bytes differ: fools "bytes changed"), dead-hidden edit, clamp edit, sensor-label shuffle (a different controller, not ecological), generalist localised edit. New negative case specific to power: a **synthetic no-signal panel** test in the library (hand-built outcome table with A = 0 and with A at 4, 5 and 6) proving `decide` returns `inconclusive` for A < 5 on either genome and that a large N with the same A fraction does not by itself change a verdict (a naive "improving rate is low so it is a null" metric would call A = 4 of 192 a no-go; the rule says inconclusive). Synthetic verdict fixtures are built from consistent row-level outcomes through `buildPayload`/`readoutFor`, never by supplying `underpowered` independently (`decide()` trusts that flag). Positive and boundary fixtures through the row-to-verdict path for both genomes: `broadly-useful` at K = ceil(10% N) and one below; `entanglement-signal` with breadth at the threshold and one below; the A = 5 boundary. The earlier phrase "same A fraction at larger N does not change a verdict" is dropped: crossing `minImproving` changes the branch, and an A = 5, K = 5 input is `broadly-useful` at N = 24 but `inconclusive` at N = 192, which a test pins. And **selection-disjointness fixtures** with independent negatives (review P2): a panel with overlapping seeds only, one with unchanged salt only, and one with a single overlapping genome hash only must each fail the disjointness check for its own reason (a combined check cannot be satisfied by the seed branch alone). **Inventory fixtures:** `recomputeProblems`/verdict construction currently accept an empty neutral-fixture set through `.every()`; the power checker requires the exact expected fixture inventory and nonempty expected sample sizes, and a test removes all fixtures and expects failure (PR136's behaviour is unchanged: the shared helper takes an expected-inventory argument that PR136 sets to its current behaviour).
2. **Panel parameterisation of the existing library (behaviour-preserving).** Thread a `Panel` record `{ id, salt, seeds, singleSlots, drawsPerMulti, secondary, allocM3, allocControl, excludeHashes, candidates: { sparse, dense }, nullNamespace }` through `buildNeighbourhood`, `drawAssaySubset`, `nullBreadths()` (which today hard-codes `${id}:null:${r}` and rebuilds neighbourhoods) and the candidate selection in `runStudy`, `AssayCache/responses/tau`, `prepare`, `runStudy`, `protocolRecord`, with `PANEL_V1` = today's constants as the default. PR136 `--replay-check` must stay byte-identical (gate). Because `tools/locality/matrix.ts --check` hashes the locality sources into `manifest.json`, editing them changes those hashes; the plan re-pins only `manifest.sources` for PR136 after the replay proves `evidence.json` is byte-identical, recorded as an explicit note in the AMENDMENT. `evidence.json` and its seal are not touched.
3. **Worker pool** in `tools/locality/pool.ts` (Deno workers; none exists, PR136 cut it). PR136 measured ~75 s serial for 24+24+8 edits, so N = 192 is roughly 8x the mutant assays, a few to ~10 minutes serial; the first gate is a baseline-only serial benchmark, and the pool is built only if serial projected time exceeds the budget (serial `--workers 1` remains the reference path either way). Tasks are (genome, edit index) assays; results are collected by index and merged in fixed order so the payload is independent of worker count; `--workers` default 2 (vitest `--maxWorkers=2` does not cap nested Deno workers; verification runs are sequential, not concurrent, and budgeted in the gates). `--replay-check` equality is asserted for 1 vs 2 workers on a small subset in a test, and for the full run in the acceptance gates.
4. **Freeze step:** `tools/locality/power.ts --freeze` computes, without any mutant assay, the panel neighbourhoods, populations, final allocation, chosen edit ids and genome hashes, generalist and M3 baselines, gate/tau, and projected runtime from timing of baseline runs. Writes `freeze.json` (sealed). Committed and pushed before the run (freeze-before-run exception).
5. **Run step:** `tools/locality/power.ts --write` refuses if `freeze.json` is missing, its seal fails, or `git` shows the freeze inputs or the power source closure (hashed in `freeze.json`) differ from `HEAD`/the pushed branch (the freeze commit is resolved externally; `freeze.json` carries source and input hashes, never its own commit hash); assays exactly the frozen edits; writes `evidence.json` + `manifest.json` (payload shape of PR136 plus `panel` and `freezeSha256`); `--check` (no simulation: seal, freeze match, recomputation of every derived field and the verdict) and `--replay-check` (regenerate and require byte equality). Reuses `checkSaved`/`recomputeProblems` by parameterising paths and protocol.
6. **Verdict and RESULT.md:** `decide()` unchanged on the new payload, applied mechanically; RESULT.md (exploratory, bounded) gives A, C, K per genome, the verdict, go/no-go, deviations, side-by-side PR136 table.
7. **Page:** extend `apps/lab/controller-locality/` with a panel selector (Original, Power-up) and a side-by-side summary table (N, A, C, K, verdict, seeds, tau); same matrix view for either panel; filter by stratum remains. Local, off the deployed site (packaging test unchanged and still passing). The power panel has about 310 generated rows per genome (2x(40+8+32+8+3) singles + 2x64 multis), not 1000. The page statically imports full evidence today, so filtering does not shrink the transfer: measure the real payload first and add a generated projection only if unreasonable (then verify summaries against full evidence). `render.ts` has no stratum/kind filter today; this slice adds one.

## 4. Files

- `tools/locality/pool.ts`, `tools/locality/power.ts`
